Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels or found yourself feeling more fatigued lately, even if you’re not doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that specifically. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything especially strenuous and getting exhausted, exactly. Does that count as depressed?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that sound like the kind of timeline you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, or found that you no longer take pleasure in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but what finally made me come in is that I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to make time for things I really cared about—painting, hiking, choir, seeing my family on Sundays—and now, even when I actually have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I can’t seem to make myself care about any of it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite, sleep, or energy levels recently? Or found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual?\n\nPatient: My sleep, yes… I mean, it’s probably not terrible compared to some people, but it’s been pretty all over the place for months. I’ll lie there awake going over grant details in my head, and then a lot of nights I wake up around three or four and can’t really settle again. Even if I technically sleep longer, I get up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels or found yourself feeling more fatigued lately, even if you’re not doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that specifically. I mean, I do feel worn out sometimes, but I’ve been assuming that’s just from not sleeping well or being busy. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s this kind of heavy gray feeling over everything. Even if I’m in the classroom with students, or at home with my husband, I can still feel sort of hollow… like I’m close to crying for no clear reason. But the truth is, painting and hiking and choir used to be things I’d make time for, and Sunday family gatherings too. Now if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there and can’t seem to care enough to start anything.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely stopped enjoying things like painting, hiking, or choir, or do you still enjoy them but just can’t seem to make yourself do them anymore?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s more that I just can’t seem to make myself do them, but honestly, even the idea of them doesn’t feel the same. Painting, hiking, choir, even Sunday family things—those used to matter to me, and I’d make time for them.\n\nNow if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I know I should care, but I can’t really get myself to. That’s what finally made me come in, I guess… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer enjoy things like painting, hiking, or choir the way you used to, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. Or found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual?\n\nPatient: My sleep, yes… I mean, it’s probably not terrible compared to some people, but it’s been pretty all over the place for months. I’ll lie there awake going over grant details in my head, and then a lot of nights I wake up around three or four and can’t really settle again. Even if I technically sleep longer, I get up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels or found yourself feeling more fatigued lately, even if you’re not doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that specifically. I mean, I do feel worn out sometimes, but I’ve been assuming that’s just from not sleeping well or being busy. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. What kind of fatigue do you mean—like physically tired, or more like no motivation?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling less energetic or more fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not completely sure. I do feel kind of drained sometimes, but I keep thinking that’s just normal with everything going on, or from not sleeping well. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything especially strenuous and getting exhausted, exactly. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most days, even if it’s not all day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s this kind of heavy gray feeling over everything. Even if I’m in the classroom with students, or at home with my husband, I can still feel sort of hollow… like I’m close to crying for no clear reason. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like painting or hiking, or do you still try to do them but just don’t get the same pleasure from them anymore?\n\nPatient: I haven’t completely stopped, exactly… or, I guess I still tell myself I could do them. But the truth is, painting and hiking and choir used to be things I’d make time for, and Sunday family gatherings too. Now if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there and can’t seem to care enough to start anything.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely stopped enjoying things like painting, hiking, or choir, or do you still enjoy them but just can’t seem to make yourself do them anymore?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s more that I just can’t seem to make myself do them, but honestly, even the idea of them doesn’t feel the same. Painting, hiking, choir, even Sunday family things—those used to matter to me, and I’d make time for them.\n\nNow if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I know I should care, but I can’t really get myself to. That’s what finally made me come in, I guess… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer enjoy things like painting, hiking, or choir the way you used to, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, because it’s not like I hate those things now, but they don’t feel like they used to.\n\nPainting, hiking, choir, even the Sunday family gatherings—I used to make time for them without really thinking about it. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, like I can’t make myself care enough to start. That’s kind of what finally brought me in… I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even when you do try to paint, hike, or join choir, you don’t get any real pleasure from them, or do they still feel enjoyable, just harder to start?\n\nPatient: I think… when I do try, it’s not that it becomes enjoyable once I get going. It’s more like I’m going through the motions, if I can even get myself to start.\n\nI used to really make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care about any of it the way I used to. I’m sorry if I’m not explaining it well, but that’s really what scared me enough to come in—I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things like painting, hiking, choir, or seeing your family on Sundays, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, “completely” sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… that’s basically what brought me in.\n\nI used to make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Those were things I cared about. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care enough to do any of it. Even when I try, it doesn’t feel like me. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel less energetic or more fatigued during the day, even when you’re not doing anything particularly tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I do feel kind of worn out sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anyone would feel during the day.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it, I guess. It’s not something I’d want to make sound bigger than it is. Are you asking more about feeling sleepy, or just having no energy?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I’ll lie there awake going over grant details in my head, and then a lot of nights I wake up around three or four and can’t really settle again. Even if I technically sleep longer, I get up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels or found yourself feeling more fatigued lately, even if you’re not doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that specifically. I mean, I do feel worn out sometimes, but I’ve been assuming that’s just from not sleeping well or being busy. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. What kind of fatigue do you mean—like physically tired, or more like no motivation?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling less energetic or more fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not completely sure. I do feel kind of drained sometimes, but I keep thinking that’s just normal with everything going on, or from not sleeping well. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything especially strenuous and getting exhausted, exactly. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most days, even if it’s not all day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s this kind of heavy gray feeling over everything. Even if I’m in the classroom with students, or at home with my husband, I can still feel sort of hollow… like I’m close to crying for no clear reason. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like painting or hiking, or do you still try to do them but just don’t get the same pleasure from them anymore?\n\nPatient: I haven’t completely stopped, exactly… or, I guess I still tell myself I could do them. But the truth is, painting and hiking and choir used to be things I’d make time for, and Sunday family gatherings too. Now if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there and can’t seem to care enough to start anything.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely stopped enjoying things like painting, hiking, or choir, or do you still enjoy them but just can’t seem to make yourself do them anymore?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s more that I just can’t seem to make myself do them, but honestly, even the idea of them doesn’t feel the same. Painting, hiking, choir, even Sunday family things—those used to matter to me, and I’d make time for them.\n\nNow if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I know I should care, but I can’t really get myself to. That’s what finally made me come in, I guess… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer enjoy things like painting, hiking, or choir the way you used to, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, because it’s not like I hate those things now, but they don’t feel like they used to.\n\nPainting, hiking, choir, even the Sunday family gatherings—I used to make time for them without really thinking about it. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, like I can’t make myself care enough to start. That’s kind of what finally brought me in… I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even when you do try to paint, hike, or join choir, you don’t get any real pleasure from them, or do they still feel enjoyable, just harder to start?\n\nPatient: I think… when I do try, it’s not that it becomes enjoyable once I get going. It’s more like I’m going through the motions, if I can even get myself to start.\n\nI used to really make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care about any of it the way I used to. I’m sorry if I’m not explaining it well, but that’s really what scared me enough to come in—I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things like painting, hiking, choir, or seeing your family on Sundays, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, “completely” sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… that’s basically what brought me in.\n\nI used to make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Those were things I cared about. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care enough to do any of it. Even when I try, it doesn’t feel like me. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel less energetic or more fatigued during the day, even when you’re not doing anything particularly tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I do feel kind of worn out sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anyone would feel during the day.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it, I guess. It’s not something I’d want to make sound bigger than it is. Are you asking more about feeling sleepy, or just having no energy?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel nothing at all when you try to do things like painting, hiking, or seeing your family, even if you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, mostly. “Nothing at all” sounds a little stark, but when I try to paint or go see my family, it’s like I’m just there. I don’t really feel connected to it the way I used to.\n\nThat’s kind of what finally made me come in. I used to make time for painting, hiking, choir, Sunday family gatherings… and now even if I have a free hour, I’ll just sit there, like I can’t make myself care about any of it. It doesn’t feel like me anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I kept telling myself it was just being busy or adjusting, but looking back, that’s about when I started feeling this way. Does that sound like the kind of timeline you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, or found that you no longer take pleasure in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but what finally made me come in is that I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I used to make time for things I really cared about—painting, hiking, choir, seeing my family on Sundays—and now, even when I actually have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I can’t seem to make myself care about any of it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite, sleep, or energy levels recently? Or found yourself feeling more guilty or worthless than usual?\n\nPatient: My sleep, yes… I mean, it’s probably not terrible compared to some people, but it’s been pretty all over the place for months. I’ll lie there awake going over grant details in my head, and then a lot of nights I wake up around three or four and can’t really settle again. Even if I technically sleep longer, I get up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels or found yourself feeling more fatigued lately, even if you’re not doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that specifically. I mean, I do feel worn out sometimes, but I’ve been assuming that’s just from not sleeping well or being busy. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. What kind of fatigue do you mean—like physically tired, or more like no motivation?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling less energetic or more fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m not completely sure. I do feel kind of drained sometimes, but I keep thinking that’s just normal with everything going on, or from not sleeping well. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything especially strenuous and getting exhausted, exactly. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most days, even if it’s not all day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days there’s this kind of heavy gray feeling over everything. Even if I’m in the classroom with students, or at home with my husband, I can still feel sort of hollow… like I’m close to crying for no clear reason. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like painting or hiking, or do you still try to do them but just don’t get the same pleasure from them anymore?\n\nPatient: I haven’t completely stopped, exactly… or, I guess I still tell myself I could do them. But the truth is, painting and hiking and choir used to be things I’d make time for, and Sunday family gatherings too. Now if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there and can’t seem to care enough to start anything.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely stopped enjoying things like painting, hiking, or choir, or do you still enjoy them but just can’t seem to make yourself do them anymore?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s more that I just can’t seem to make myself do them, but honestly, even the idea of them doesn’t feel the same. Painting, hiking, choir, even Sunday family things—those used to matter to me, and I’d make time for them.\n\nNow if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there. I know I should care, but I can’t really get myself to. That’s what finally made me come in, I guess… I don’t feel like myself anymore. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer enjoy things like painting, hiking, or choir the way you used to, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to overstate it, because it’s not like I hate those things now, but they don’t feel like they used to.\n\nPainting, hiking, choir, even the Sunday family gatherings—I used to make time for them without really thinking about it. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, like I can’t make myself care enough to start. That’s kind of what finally brought me in… I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that even when you do try to paint, hike, or join choir, you don’t get any real pleasure from them, or do they still feel enjoyable, just harder to start?\n\nPatient: I think… when I do try, it’s not that it becomes enjoyable once I get going. It’s more like I’m going through the motions, if I can even get myself to start.\n\nI used to really make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care about any of it the way I used to. I’m sorry if I’m not explaining it well, but that’s really what scared me enough to come in—I don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things like painting, hiking, choir, or seeing your family on Sundays, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, “completely” sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… that’s basically what brought me in.\n\nI used to make time for painting, hiking, choir, and seeing family on Sundays. Those were things I cared about. Now even if I have a free hour, I mostly just sit there, and I can’t seem to care enough to do any of it. Even when I try, it doesn’t feel like me. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel less energetic or more fatigued during the day, even when you’re not doing anything particularly tiring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I do feel kind of worn out sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anyone would feel during the day.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it, I guess. It’s not something I’d want to make sound bigger than it is. Are you asking more about feeling sleepy, or just having no energy?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel nothing at all when you try to do things like painting, hiking, or seeing your family, even if you manage to start them?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, mostly. “Nothing at all” sounds a little stark, but when I try to paint or go see my family, it’s like I’m just there. I don’t really feel connected to it the way I used to.\n\nThat’s kind of what finally made me come in. I used to make time for painting, hiking, choir, Sunday family gatherings… and now even if I have a free hour, I’ll just sit there, like I can’t make myself care about any of it. It doesn’t feel like me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble keeping up with your responsibilities at work or home because of how you’ve been feeling?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I wish I could say I’m managing it better than I am.\n\nAt work, I’ve missed several teaching sessions, and I’ve been avoiding some of the student workshops because I just don’t feel like I can keep up with the responsibilities the way I used to. I don’t want it to sound like I don’t care about the students—I do. I just… I get overwhelmed and pull back.\n\nAnd at home, my husband has had to step in more. I’ve been withdrawing from family plans, and sometimes I don’t feel safe trying to just push through it by myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
